- **Text input blinking cursor and parent-child focus hierarchy (`hl2-ui::gameui::widgets`, `hl2-ui::gameui::options`, `hl2-ui::console`):**
  - Implemented `InputFocus` in `crates/hl2-ui/src/gameui/widgets.rs` modeling parent-child focus relations (`parent_focused: bool` and `child_focused: bool`). `is_active(&self) -> bool` returns `true` only when both parent dialog and child input element hold focus.
  - Implemented `BlinkingCaret` providing Source SDK `TextEntry` 2.0 Hz caret blinking cycle (500ms visible, 500ms hidden). Gated via `is_visible(focus: InputFocus, time: f64)`.
  - Implemented `TextInputModel` combining `InputFocus`, `BlinkingCaret`, and optional interaction timestamp for resetting caret blink phase on typing/interaction (`record_interaction`). Separated continuous `should_draw_focus_edge()` (focus ring) from periodic `should_draw_caret(time)` (1px insertion cursor).
  - Updated `Painter::text_entry` to accept separate `focused: bool` and `caret_visible: bool` parameters, and added `Painter::text_entry_model(rect, text, enabled, model: &TextInputModel, time: f64)`.
  - In `OptionsDialog` (`crates/hl2-ui/src/gameui/options.rs`), added `pub parent_focused: bool` and `pub now: Option<f64>`. In `draw_with_occluders`, resolved effective parent focus (`parent_focused && !modal`, false when SubDialog or Confirmation is active) and passed `time` and `parent_focused` down to controls. In `ControlKind::TextEntry`, constructed `TextInputModel::new(parent_focused, child_focused)` where `child_focused` is true when the entry is actively being edited, rendering the blinking caret via `p.text_entry_model`. Slider text entries (e.g. Mouse sensitivity) blink at 2.0 Hz when focused/editing and hide immediately when unfocused, when Options loses focus to another window, or when an overlying subdialog or confirmation box is open.
  - In `Console` (`crates/hl2-ui/src/console.rs`), synchronized `options.parent_focused = self.windows.focus() == Layer::Window(Window::Options)`. In `draw_console_with_occluders`, evaluated `console_focused = self.windows.focus() == Layer::Window(Window::Console)` and `entry_focused = !self.console_placement.active()`, rendering `entry_focus_edge` and blinking caret via `TextInputModel`. Caret and focus edge hide when console is out of focus.
  - Documented Section 22 ("Text input with blinking cursor and parent-child focus hierarchy") in `docs/gameui.md`.
  - Verified with unit tests (`widgets::tests::input_focus_parent_child_relations`, `widgets::tests::blinking_caret_phase_timing_and_focus_gating`, `widgets::tests::text_input_model_and_interaction_restart`, `widgets::tests::painter_text_entry_focus_edge_and_caret_drawing`, `options::tests::options_slider_text_entry_cursor_blinks_when_focused_and_hides_when_unfocused`, `console::tests::console_entry_cursor_blinks_when_focused_and_hides_when_unfocused`), `scripts/checks/validate.sh` (351 hl2-ui unit tests, 93 hl2-bevy tests, strict clippy, rustfmt check), and `scripts/checks/check_windows.sh`. Visual appearance in packaged release runs is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Subdialog and confirmation focus styling and reversible theme transitions (`hl2-ui::gameui::widgets`, `hl2-ui::gameui::options`, `hl2-ui::console`):**
  - Added `frame_bg_focused: Color` and `title_text_focused: Color` to `Theme` (`crates/hl2-ui/src/gameui/widgets.rs`), initialized in `Theme::default()` and recorded from `sourcescheme.res` in `Theme::from_scheme()`.
  - In `Theme::focus_faded(fade, focused)`, blend `frame_bg` from `self.frame_bg_out_of_focus` to `self.frame_bg_focused` (preserving focused colors), restoring `title_text_focused` when `focused` is true. Any theme can now transition to out-of-focus and back to focused without losing its authentic scheme colors.
  - In `OptionsDialog` (`crates/hl2-ui/src/gameui/options.rs`), added `pub sub_theme: Option<Theme>` and `pub confirm_theme: Option<Theme>`. In `draw_with_occluders`, when a subdialog holds focus, its frame renders with `Frame.BgColor` (alpha 212) and focused title text `FrameTitleBar.TextColor` (white), while the underlying Options dialog renders with `Frame.OutOfFocusBgColor` (alpha 62) and `FrameTitleBar.DisabledTextColor`. When confirmation opens on top of a subdialog, confirmation renders in focus while the subdialog transitions to out-of-focus (`Frame.OutOfFocusBgColor` / alpha 62).
  - In `Console::sync_dialog_windows` (`crates/hl2-ui/src/console.rs`), synchronized `self.options.sub_theme` and `self.options.confirm_theme` from `self.window_theme(Window::SubDialog, time)` and `self.window_theme(Window::Confirmation, time)`.
  - Verified with unit tests (`widgets::tests::focus_faded_transitions_are_reversible_and_preserve_focused_colors`, `gameui::options::tests::dialogs_over_the_options_dialog_are_translucent_and_clip_underlying_content`, `console::tests::subdialog_in_focus_renders_focused_frame_while_parent_options_is_unfocused`), `scripts/checks/validate.sh` (345 hl2-ui unit tests, 93 hl2-bevy tests, strict clippy, rustfmt check), and `scripts/checks/check_windows.sh`. Visual appearance in packaged release runs is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Stacked dialog translucency with content occlusion clipping and modal console toggle gating (`hl2-ui::canvas`, `hl2-ui::gameui::widgets`, `hl2-ui::gameui::options`, `hl2-ui::console`):**
  - Removed 100% opacity logic for stacked dialogs (`Theme::stacked()` no longer overrides `frame_bg.a = 1.0`). All dialogs out of focus or stacked over another dialog preserve their authentic translucent scheme background (`Frame.BgColor` / `Frame.OutOfFocusBgColor` from `sourcescheme.res`).
  - Added an occluder stack (`push_occluder`, `pop_occluder`, `clear_occluders`) to `Canvas`. In `Canvas::quad`, quads intersecting active occluders are clipped via 2D axis-aligned box subtraction (`clip_quad_against_occluder`) with proportional source texture UV scaling, removing covered regions. Polygons with all vertices inside an occluder are dropped. `Canvas::drain` clears active occluders between frames.
  - Split `Painter::frame_spec` into `frame_background(&spec)` (unclipped frame fill and outer border) and `frame_foreground(&spec, close)` (title text and close button).
  - In `OptionsDialog::draw_with_occluders`, the Options frame background is drawn first unclipped, then occluders for any overlying subdialog (`sub_bounds`), confirmation box (`confirm_bounds`), or external window are pushed before drawing the foreground title, close button, tabs, controls, and bottom buttons. Subdialogs similarly draw their frame background unclipped, then push occluders for overlying confirmation boxes before drawing subdialog content.
  - In `Console::draw_console_with_occluders`, the console frame background is drawn unclipped, then occluders for overlying dialogs (e.g. Quit confirmation box) are pushed before drawing console history, entry, submit button, and completion list.
  - In `Console::toggle()`, added guard checking `self.windows.has_modal() || self.modal_dialog_open() || self.options.has_sub() || self.options.has_confirm()`. Pressing tilde (`KeyCode::Backquote`), `Action::ToggleConsole`, or `toggleconsole` while in Options > Video > Advanced, Gamma, Credits, Keyboard Advanced, or a confirmation dialog blocks console toggle invocation early.
  - Verified with unit tests (`canvas::tests::occluder_clips_quads_and_proportional_texture_coordinates`, `gameui::options::tests::dialogs_over_the_options_dialog_are_translucent_and_clip_underlying_content`, `console::tests::console_invocation_is_blocked_when_blocking_subdialog_or_modal_is_open`), `scripts/checks/validate.sh`, and `scripts/checks/check_windows.sh`. Visual appearance in packaged release runs is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Confirmation dialogs arm Cancel button on held Escape (`hl2-ui::gameui::menu`, `hl2-ui::console`, `hl2-ui::gameui::options`):**
  - Added `escape_held: bool` to `MessageBox`. In `MessageBox::draw`, when `escape_held` is active and the dialog has a Cancel button without a title-bar close button (`!has_close`), the Cancel button renders with `armed: true` and `pressed: true` (`ButtonState::depressed`), applying sunken 3D borders, 1px text offset, and depressed styling.
  - In `Console::escape_pressed`, synchronized dialog windows and propagated `escape_held = true` to `confirm_box` and `options.confirm` when `Window::Confirmation` is top.
  - In `Console::escape_released`, cleared `escape_held` and dismissed the confirmation dialog upon release. Dragging confirmation boxes cancels active drags on Escape press without holding Cancel.
  - Verified with unit tests (`message_box_escape_held_arms_cancel_when_no_close_button`, `a_held_escape_presses_confirmation_cancel_button_and_release_cancels_it`), `scripts/checks/validate.sh`, and `scripts/checks/check_windows.sh`. Visual appearance in packaged release runs is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Focused subdialog close button highlights on held Escape (`hl2-ui::console`):**
  - Updated `Console::escape_pressed` to set `options.escape_held = true` when `windows.top()` is `Some(Window::SubDialog)`, causing `OptionsDialog::draw` to render the subdialog's title-bar close button in pressed/highlighted state while Escape is held.
  - Delegated `Window::SubDialog` escape handling in `Console::escape_closes_dialog` and `Console::escape` to `options.escape_closes()` and `options.escape()`, ensuring active drags and open combo dropdowns are cancelled first without prematurely closing the subdialog.
  - Verified with unit test `console::tests::a_held_escape_presses_subdialog_close_button_and_release_closes_it`, `scripts/checks/validate.sh`, and `scripts/checks/check_windows.sh`. Visual appearance in packaged release runs is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Options sub-dialog mouse clicks, Use Defaults confirmation, and out-of-focus title translucency (`hl2-ui::console`, `gameui::options`, `gameui::settings`):**
  - Synchronized dialog window input routing in `Console::windows_input` to route mouse clicks strictly to `active_options_window` (`Confirmation` > `SubDialog` > `Options`), preventing lower windows from wiping `ButtonTracker` state.
  - Added `"apply"` command mapping in `gameui::settings::command_binding` for `OptionsSubVideoAdvancedDlg.res`.
  - Retained `confirm` across input frames in `OptionsDialog::input` when pending outcome is `None`.
  - Modulated `title_outline` and `sub_outline` with `base_focus` in `Console::draw`, disabling black text outline when out of focus so title and subtitle glyphs render with pure translucency.
  - Verified with unit tests (`subdialog_buttons_can_be_activated_by_mouse_click`, `keyboard_use_defaults_confirmation_works_with_click_and_keys`, `out_of_focus_menu_title_and_subtitle_are_translucent_without_black_outline`), `scripts/checks/validate.sh` and `scripts/checks/check_windows.sh`. Visual appearance in packaged release runs is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Mid-gameplay developer console opens pause menu (`hl2-ui::console`, `hl2-bevy::simulation`):**
  - Opening the developer console mid-gameplay (`toggle()`, `set_mode(Mode::Console)`) automatically transitions base to `Mode::Pause`, bringing up the authentic pause menu with dev console focused on top.
  - Closing the developer console mid-gameplay (tilde, Escape, or `[X]` button) restores focus to the underlying pause menu (`Layer::Base`), leaving gameplay paused.
  - Resuming from the pause menu (Escape or "Resume Game") returns cleanly to active gameplay (`Mode::Gameplay`).
  - Unit-tested via `console::tests::mid_gameplay_tilde_dev_console_transition_and_pause_menu_focus`, `simulation::tests::ui_open_close_and_resume_consume_pointer_attack_and_held_jump`, `validate.sh`, and `check_windows.sh`. Visual backdrop in packaged runs is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Context-aware background dim overlay (menu background vs. mid-gameplay) (`hl2-ui::console`, `tuning.json`):**
  - Added `backdrop_alpha: 0.611765` matching `MainMenu.Backdrop` in `sourcescheme.res` (`156 / 255`) and `gameplay_dim_alpha: 0.3` (unverified fallback) in `assets/ui/tuning.json`.
  - Implemented pure calculation in `Console::dim_overlay_opacity(is_mid_gameplay, menu_open, base_focus) -> f32` and `Console::dim_overlay_alpha(time)`.
  - Menu background mode: zero dim overlay when main/sub menu is focused, smoothly fades in to `MainMenu.Backdrop` (0.6118) over 0.3s when dialogs open.
  - Mid-gameplay mode: pause menu applies `gameplay_dim_alpha` (0.3) over gameplay; dialogs opening on top smoothly transition dim to `MainMenu.Backdrop` (0.6118).
  - Verified with unit tests (`console::tests::context_aware_background_dim_overlay_across_all_four_cases`), `scripts/checks/validate.sh`, and `scripts/checks/check_windows.sh`. Visual smoothness is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Shared fade-in/out and focus-transition animations (`gameui::windows`, `canvas`, `console`, `gameui::options`):**
  - Verified `sourcescheme.res` values: `Frame.TransitionEffectTime` = 0.3 s, `Frame.FocusTransitionEffectTime` = 0.3 s, `Frame.BgColor` alpha = 212, `Frame.OutOfFocusBgColor` alpha = 62.
  - Implemented `Canvas::set_alpha_multiplier` and `Canvas::alpha_multiplier` (matching Source SDK `surface()->DrawSetAlphaMultiplier()`), modulating drawing primitives across dialogs, text, and widgets.
  - Added `WindowRecord` in `gameui::windows` tracking `focus_fade`, `alpha_fade`, and `closing: bool`.
  - Closing dialogs fade out visibility alpha from current down to 0.0 over 0.3 s; focus and interaction immediately hand off to the underlying surface without delay, and finished closing dialogs are purged automatically via `Windows::clean_closed()`.
  - Unified composite alpha calculation: $A_{\text{comp}} = A_{\text{vis}} \times (u + (1 - u) \times F)$ with $u = 0.3$.
  - Unit-tested via `shared_fade_state_and_interpolation_logic_with_deterministic_timesteps`, `validate.sh`, and `check_windows.sh`. Visual smoothness is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Unified single-focus state across menus, dialogs, and sub-dialogs (`gameui::windows`, `console`, `gameui::options`):**
  - Enforced single-focus invariant in `Windows::focus()`: strictly one layer (`Layer::Base` or `Layer::Window(top)`) holds keyboard and primary interaction focus at any time.
  - Sub-dialogs and confirmation dialogs integrated into `Windows` enum as modal layers (`Window::SubDialog`, `Window::Confirmation`), synchronized through `Console::sync_dialog_windows()`.
  - When sub-dialogs or confirmation dialogs open, parent dialogs lose focus and transition to translucent (`Theme::focus_faded` using `Frame.OutOfFocusBgColor`).
  - When any dialog opens over the main/sub menu, the main menu loses focus: the title, subtitle, and menu buttons fade to translucent using `base_focus_amount(time)` and `unfocused_alpha_factor: 0.3` (matching `sourcescheme.res` `Frame.OutOfFocusBgColor`).
  - Closing dialogs restores focus step by step back to the next parent dialog, or to the main menu when all dialogs are dismissed.
  - Verified with unit tests (`single_focus_state_machine_across_menus_dialogs_and_subdialogs`, `main_menu_loses_focus_and_becomes_translucent_when_dialog_opens_and_restores_on_close`), `scripts/checks/validate.sh` and `scripts/checks/check_windows.sh`. Visual smoothness is unverified, needs manual check.

- **Automated target cleaner & build verification (`scripts/checks/clean_target.py`, `scripts/clean-target.sh`, `launch-bevy.sh`):**
  - Added standalone cleaner supporting `--stale` (default: prunes orphaned incremental sessions, stale `.rcgu.o` object files, old test runners and superseded `.rlib` archives for workspace crates, leaving active builds and 3rd-party crates intact), `--workspace` (`cargo clean -p ...` without touching Bevy/wgpu/Rapier), and `--dry-run`.
  - Integrated into `launch-bevy.sh`: added `--clean` flag and auto-pruning of stale hashes whenever a git branch change is detected.
  - Reclaimed 29.07 GiB of dead build remnants on disk (119,029 stale files and 68 dead incremental directories removed; `target/` reduced from 29+ GB to 9.3 GB).
  - Verified with dry-run checks, `scripts/checks/validate.sh` and `scripts/checks/check_windows.sh`.

- **Viewmodel sequence transitions & cross-fading (fix crowbar swing twitch/hop):**
  - In `hl2-simulation::viewmodel_effects`, added `ViewmodelAnimationState` (`Playing`, `Transition`, `Idle`), `viewmodel_animation_state`, `viewmodel_state_matrices`, and `Inventory::viewmodel_matrices`.
  - Finishing non-looping sequences (such as crowbar `misscenter1`, pistol `fire`, shotgun `fire01`/`pump`) now cross-fade smoothly into the active weapon's idle animation over `fade_time = min(clip.fade_out, idle.fade_in)` (default 0.2s) using Source SDK `MaintainSequenceTransitions` smoothstep spline blending ($s = 3s^2 - 2s^3$), holding the finished sequence at its final frame while advancing idle.
  - Unified viewmodel matrix evaluation across `hl2-bevy::rendering` (joint transforms and CPU skinning) and `hl2-bevy::effects` (sprites, quads and beams), preventing discrete 10.7-unit bone jumps when animations expire.
  - Verified with unit tests (`viewmodel_effects::tests::viewmodel_animation_state_transitions_smoothly_to_idle`), owned-install regression test (`gameplay::tests::owned_crowbar_attack_transition_is_smooth_without_twitch_or_hop`), `validate.sh`, and `check_windows.sh`.
- **Dynamic/flexible aspect ratio ("Fit Display"):** Options > Video > Aspect Ratio adds "Fit Display" (`AspectRatio::Flexible`, `video.flexible_aspect`), dynamically shaping the game frame to window, display, or workspace bounds.
  - Universal aspect-ratio math (`hl2-ui::tuning`): `min_supported_aspect` (9:16) and `max_supported_aspect` (32:9); `clamp_supported_aspect`; `cubic_bezier_ease` ($3t^2 - 2t^3$) providing $C^1$-smooth extrapolation between known aspect ratios with zero slope at plateau boundaries; `aspect_factors` smoothly blending portrait and ultrawide strengths for world FOV and viewmodel framing.
  - Dynamic frame resolution & viewport (`hl2-ui::viewport`, `hl2-bevy::video`): `flexible_frame_resolution` sizes the frame to the active window/display within 9:16..=32:9 at the detail level of the configured short side; `ViewportBounds::compute_with_mode` and `VideoViewport::compute_with_mode` fill 100% of the active window/display with 0 black bars in 9:16..=32:9 and letterbox/pillarbox only outside that range.
  - Windowed mode: `flexible_windowed_size` limits each dimension independently to workspace bounds (`DesktopArea::client_room` on macOS, monitor bounds on Windows/Linux) without forcing uniform aspect shrinking; live window resizes dynamically reshape the rendering frame.
  - Auto-resolution & disabled Resolution dropdown: when "Fit Display" is selected and active, the Resolution combo is disabled (`settings::enabled` returns false when `draft.aspect == AspectRatio::Flexible`, which also disables its associated label `ResolutionLabel`) and displays the active content resolution in physical pixels without letterbox or pillarbox black stripes (`"{w} x {h}"`, e.g. `"1920 x 1080"`, via `current.without_stripes_for_flexible()`); selecting from it is a no-op, and switching back to fixed aspect ratios re-enables Resolution.
  - All 93 checks in `replays.py` passed; `validate.sh` and `check_windows.sh` passed.
- **Aspect-adaptive automatic world/vertical FOV:** `tuning::world_fov_offset(aspect)` tunes automatic world FOV outside 5:4..=16:9 (`world_fov_offset` in `tuning.json`: portrait -17°, ultrawide -11°), reducing vertical fish-eye distortion on portrait (down to 9:16) and peripheral horizontal stretching on ultrawide (up to 32:9).
- **Aspect-adaptive and per-weapon viewmodel framing:** `tuning::viewmodel_framing` adapts first-person arms and weapons outside 5:4..=16:9 using per-weapon and default profiles in `tuning.json` (offsets and FOV adjustments for gravity gun, RPG, crowbar).

## Previous session: Half-Life 2 submenu background (2026-10-16, branch `orbitalman/main-menu-2`)

Claude Code desktop, claude-opus-5-5.
- **Menu backgrounds use their `point_viewcontrol` camera and `env_zoom` FOV.** Once the world ran, the Episode Two view fell to the ground: the view was the player's eye and its spawn is not under the camera. Every chapter background enables a `point_viewcontrol` (`view_controller`, spawnflags 28: freeze player, infinite hold, snap) from a `logic_auto`, looking at `view_target` (Half-Life 2, Lost Coast) or along its `angles` (episodes); Half-Life 2 ones lock FOV 75 with `env_zoom`. New `hl2_simulation::view_control` (SDK `CTriggerCamera` Enable/Disable, `OnEndFollow`, target look-at, finite `wait` return, freeze; `CEnvZoom` Zoom/UnZoom with rate blending); the host renders, culls, mixes audio and draws effects from `Simulation::view_origin`/`view_angles`/`view_fov`, freezes the player under spawnflag 4, and keeps selecting soundscapes from the player's eye (SDK `EarPosition`). Tests: `view_control` unit tests; owned `chapter_background_levels_run_live_behind_the_menu` now asserts all 15 views sit at their camera and the HL2 FOV is 75. Visual check (`scripts/checks/replays.py` (`bg_compare`), 1024x768, FOV 75) against the retail pre-blurred menu images: HL2 `background01` and `ep1_background01` line up, `ep2_background01` and Lost Coast match in layout (Lost Coast's image has its horizon a few degrees higher and shows mountains/water our render lacks). Seen while comparing, not fixed: magenta missing-material areas (ep2 ground, Lost Coast water).
- **Menu background levels run live (ambient sounds and soundscapes play).** Before, the main menu counted as a pause, so the background world never ticked and no ambient or soundscape sound started. Like retail, a background level now keeps running behind the menu with player input ignored (SDK `in_main.cpp`): `Simulation::world_running()` (`!loading && (!paused || background_level)`, `background_level` read from the console every step) drives the world tick, audio sink pausing, effects and tonemap adaptation; `paused()` still governs input, cursor and respawn. Entities on the background animate too. Tests: unit `background_level_world_runs_behind_the_menu_without_player_input` (time advances, forward/attack ignored; the gameplay pause menu advances nothing); owned `chapter_background_levels_run_live_behind_the_menu` (all 15 chapter backgrounds of hl2/episodic/ep2/lostcoast run 10 s with no level change, death or load; each game's menu background has a soundscape or spawn-playing ambient; `ep1_background02` and `ep2_background02` select no soundscape at the spawn, as their only in-range data says). Packaged: plain launch over Lost Coast's menu, report `world_running: true`, soundscape `coast_lostcoast.general_shoreline`, 4 sinks playing, 0 paused; `d1_trainstation_02` + Escape: `world_running: false`, 4/4 sinks paused. Not checked by ear by me; not run on Windows.
- **Search paths come from each game's `gameinfo.txt`.** `Vfs::mount_game` mounts the `FileSystem/SearchPaths` the game declares (`vpk::search_paths`: `game`/`mod`/`platform` entries in order, `|gameinfo_path|` and `|all_source_engine_paths|` resolved, `<folder>/*` expanded, `<name>.vpk` opened as `<name>_dir.vpk`, missing entries and repeats skipped); the hardcoded `mount_expansion` table (`ep2` -> `episodic`) and the separate custom-folder pass are gone. Retail order puts every VPK before every loose folder; a search-order check found no game file shadowed by that (only loose `hl2/models/weapons/*.mdl` duplicates now come from `hl2_misc`, as in retail). Tests: `games_mount_the_search_paths_their_gameinfo_declares`, owned `owned_install_games_mount_vpks_before_loose_folders`; all owned `source-assets` tests (installation discovered like the runtime, no `HL2_ROOT` needed), `hl2-simulation` with `--include-ignored`, owned `hl2-ui`/`hl2-bevy` tests pass.
- **Return to main menu showed the previous submenu's background: fixed.** From the Episode Two menu, `map d1_trainstation_02` then Return to main menu came back over `ep2_background01`. It now shows the running game's chapter 1 background and saves it (`Console::menu_background_after_game`). Unit `return_to_main_menu_shows_the_running_games_background`; packaged replay `scripts/checks/replays.py` (`return_menu`): `hl2/background01`, `ep2/ep2_background01`, `hl2/d1_trainstation_02`, `hl2/background01`.
- **`scripts/build-bevy.sh` replaces `bin/hl2-bevy` instead of overwriting it.** Overwriting the executable in place while an earlier build was still running made macOS kill every new launch (exit 137, empty log).
- **The game of a map is carried, not guessed from its name.** Submenus already know their game (`SwitchExpansion <game>`), so `Effect::MainMenuMap` carries a `console::GameMap { game, map }` through the campaign request to `assets::load_with_canvas(root, game, map, ..)`, which mounts that game (`Vfs::mount_game`); `LoadedMap`, `Campaign::loaded_map`, `MapLoaded` and the run report carry the game; the persisted background keeps `gameplay.background_game`. Background images and transitions are keyed by `(game, map)`, so Lost Coast's `background01` needs no alias and a stale Half-Life 2 `background01` completion no longer finishes a Lost Coast transition. Bare map names (`map`, `map_background`, `--map`, `--background-map`, respawn, a background saved before games were recorded) resolve from assets: the game whose own folder ships `maps/<name>.bsp`, loose or in its own VPKs, `hl2` first, then the other `gameinfo.txt` folders (`source_assets::install::game_for_map`); in-session names (`map`, `map_background`, respawn) search the running game first, command-line names do not. Level transitions stay in the running game. Removed: `vpk::expansion_for_map`, `Console::expansion_category_for_map`, the `lostcoast_background01` key and its disk-name translation. Tests: unit `game_for_map_reads_each_game_folder_base_game_first`, `background_images_are_keyed_by_game_and_map`, `background_transition_tells_games_with_the_same_map_name_apart`; owned-install `owned_install_maps_belong_to_the_game_that_ships_them`, `background01_loads_from_the_named_game` (Half-Life 2 and Lost Coast `background01` load different worlds), headless Lost Coast submenu switch. Packaged replay (`scripts/checks/replays.py` (`submenus`), now Episode One -> back -> Half-Life 2 -> back -> Extras -> Lost Coast): `hl2/background01`, `episodic/ep1_background01`, `hl2/background01`, `lostcoast/background01`; a saved `ep2_background01` without a game started on `ep2/ep2_background01`. Not changed: `load_vtf_file` keeps its per-game archive order (it also reads `hl2_complete`, which no game's search path mounts). (The `mount_expansion` table was replaced afterwards, see above.)
- **Half-Life 2 submenu did not switch the background: fixed.** Root cause: `GameUiResources` is built once from the startup map's file system, which is mounted for the game of the saved background (for example Episode Two). Half-Life 2's entry then read `scripts/chapterbackgrounds.txt` through that mount and got the episode's copy, so HL2 resolved to the episode background already showing and nothing loaded (New Game maps had the same flaw through `cfg/chapter1.cfg`). Now each game reads its own chapter files through its own mount (`resources::read_game_text`), and the games come from the `SwitchExpansion` commands of `gamemenu.res` (`resources::menu_games`); the hardcoded `INITIAL_BACKGROUND_MAPS` / `INITIAL_EPISODE_MAPS` tables and their fallbacks are gone. Tests: owned-install `every_game_reads_its_own_chapter_files_whatever_map_is_loaded` (base, Episode One, Episode Two and Lost Coast startup mounts; fails when reading through the startup mount), `menu_games_come_from_switch_expansion_commands_in_menu_order`. Packaged replay (`scripts/checks/replays.py` (`submenus`), Episode One -> back -> Half-Life 2): before, HL2 loaded `ep2_background01`; after, `ep1_background01` then `background01`. (The name-based mount and the `lostcoast_background01` key were removed afterwards, see above.)

 ## Previous session: Background map transition state sequence and dimming effect (2026-10-15, branch `orbitalman/main-menu`)

Antigravity, Gemini 3.8 Flash model family.
- **Background map transition state sequence & dimming effect:** Modeled background map loading as an explicit state sequence and hardened failure cleanup:
  - In `hl2-ui::gameui::loading`, added `BackgroundTransitionState` (`Idle`, `Starting { map }`, `Loading { map, elapsed }`, `Finished { map }`, `Failed { map, reason }`) with methods `start`, `update`, `finish`, `fail`, `reset`, `is_active`, `is_terminal`, `is_failed`, `is_finished`, `target_map`, and `overlay_alpha`.
  - In `hl2-ui::gameui::loading`, embedded `transition: BackgroundTransitionState` on `LoadingDialog`. In `new_background`, initializes transition to `Starting { map }`. In `update`, advances transition elapsed time. In `set_error`, transitions to `Failed { reason }`. In `set_complete`, transitions to `Finished`.
  - In `hl2-ui::gameui::loading`, updated `draw_background_overlay` and `draw` (corner plaque) to only emit quads while `transition.is_active()` is true.
  - Added unit tests: `test_background_transition_success_sequence`, `test_background_transition_failure_sequence`, and `test_background_loading_dialog_failure_ends_transition_and_suppresses_overlay`.
  - In `hl2-ui::console`, added `background_transition: BackgroundTransitionState` to `Console`. Added `Console::background_load_failed` and updated `Console::loading_failed` to transition to `Failed` and dismiss the dialog and plaque. Updated `Console::level_loaded` and `Console::loading_complete` to mark active transitions as `Finished`. Added `Console::background_transition_state`.
  - Added unit tests: `background_loading_state_sequence_success`, `background_load_failed_ends_transition_and_cleans_up`, and `generic_loading_failed_on_background_cleans_up`.
  - In `hl2-bevy::campaign`, updated `step_map_load` on background load failure to invoke `ui.source.background_load_failed(&error.to_string())`, cleanly registering failure in the transition state machine while keeping the menu intact.
  - Root cause analysis: in `hl2-bevy`, HUD material quads do not sample the active 3D framebuffer (no intermediate render target or blur pass). True live scene blur in Source requires multi-pass offscreen render targets (`BlurFilterX/Y`), which is part of the 3D pipeline rather than 2D VGUI. Per guidance, the contained effect retains the authentic dimming overlay and non-blocking plaque while modeling the transition as an explicit, robust state sequence.
  - Documented findings in `AGENTS.md`.
- **Validation:** 157 `hl2-ui` tests pass, all workspace unit tests pass; all owned-install tests pass; headless Bevy app test passes in 2.08s; clippy clean (0 warnings), fmt clean.

In `Console::draw`, updated menu button rendering (`Mode::MainMenu` and `Mode::Pause`) to draw non-spacer entry labels with `draw_cell_text_outlined` using a thin semi-transparent black outline (`TextOutline::new(border_color, 1.0)`, where `border_color` is `Color::new(0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.45)` default / `0.65` armed / `0.25` disabled). No rectangular bounding boxes, fills, or panels are drawn on the button clickable area. When pressed, the letter text and its contour outline shift together by `(1.0, 1.0)`. Diagnosed two root causes: (1) `Vfs::read` previously iterated all loose search paths before checking VPK archives, causing loose base `hl2/scripts/chapterbackgrounds.txt` to shadow expansion VPKs (`ep1_pak_dir.vpk`) during resource load; refactored `Vfs` into layered `SearchPath { root, archives }` so each mount layer resolves loose files and archives together in authentic Source order. The Lost Coast entry opens the Lost Coast game submenu adhering to the standard 6-row game submenu structure (New Game, Load Game placeholder, Achievements placeholder, spacer, Options, Back) with data-driven initial map `d2_lostcoast`, background map `background01`, and authentic title. Message boxes default to `has_close = false` per original SDK `vgui::MessageBox` rules, with `with_close(bool)` builder support and Cancel routing. - **(a) Evidence tools (native; packaged).** Native: the private view oracle takes ORACLE_RAW (no noclip, notarget or HUD changes) and `!raw` views (no setpos, setang or fov), and artifacts/s56/native_firstmap.py records a d1_trainstation_01 time-lapse from map load with the player left alone (artifacts/s56/native-ts01: 44 frames 7-229 s, times in times.json; settings unchanged). Ours: `--capture-every <seconds>` saves the game frame each interval of scene time in one run; movement scripts may run to tick 40000 and `--frames` to 200000 (they stopped at 6000 and 36000).
- **(b) A New Game of d1_trainstation_01 went black after the G-Man shots (native; packaged).** Ours stayed in the intro room: point_teleport, func_tracktrain/path_track and script_intro had no implementation (also env_credits, env_message, env_starfield, env_sprite, func_train), and env_zoom treated FOV 0 as zero degrees, so zoom_final (FOV 0 over 14 s, meant to ease back to the default FOV) zoomed in forever (the owner's observation).
- **(c) The train ride (SDK; packaged).** New trains.rs and entities/tracktrain.rs: func_tracktrain on path_track per trains.cpp/pathtrack.cpp (Find, Next with LookAhead 0.1 s ahead, OnPass of each node reached, NOCONTROL node speeds, facing `wheels` ahead, dead ends; Stop, StartForward/Backward, Toggle, Resume, Reverse, SetSpeed, SetSpeedReal, SetSpeedDir; path_track InPass, EnablePath, DisablePath, TogglePath); point_teleport (Teleport to its origin and angles, SF_TELEPORT_TO_SPAWN_POS); trains are solid brush entities (physics/brush_entities.rs, SF_TRACKTRAIN_PASSABLE not solid); the host carries a grounded player on the train it stands on and turns the view by the train's yaw (simulation/ride.rs; CPhysicsPushedEntities, FinishRotPushedEntity). Unit tests: a train placed on its first node, 100 u/s along the path, OnPass, the turn, the dead end, the carry; point_teleport. Packaged (artifacts/s56/ours-ts03-sheet.png): the player is teleported into intro_train_2 at 61 s, rides it and arrives at about 90 s, the same corridor view and timing as native's 64.7-91.4 s frames. Not in: NPC riders (citizen_train_1/2), train sounds, blocked trains, alternate paths, TeleportToPathTrack, the blend orientation types (they face like AtPathTracks).
- **(d) env_zoom (SDK; packaged).** Zoom now goes through the player's SetFOV ramp (FovRamp, already used by the crossbow): the start is the current FOV, SimpleSpline over the rate, FOV 0 is the default FOV, and a finished return to the default lets the view go. The intro's zoom chain (1, 40 over 12 s, 10, 45 over 15 s, 30 over 10 s, 42, then the default over 14 s) now eases like native instead of jumping from the player's FOV.
- **(e) script_intro (SDK; packaged).** New script_intro.rs: the state machine (blend modes and timed blends with BlendComplete, FOV and FOV blends, FadeTo, SetFadeColor, SetCameraViewEntity, Activate/Deactivate) as a pure function of time; its fade replaces the screen fade while non-zero (fades.rs, GetFadeParams). intro_view.rs renders the camera entity's view into a frame-sized image while the intro is active and has one, and a pass after the world camera's tonemapping blends the two pictures with IntroScreenSpaceEffect's modes in gamma space (assets/shaders/intro_composite.wgsl; the motion blur and the intro share bloom/fullscreen_pass.rs). point_viewcontrol looks at its target's `targetattachment` from the actor's animated pose. After that (artifacts/s56/ours-ts06-sheet.png): the HL1 room's glow over the mouth (25-35 s, native 27.5/32.4), G-Man over the moving train interior (38-50 s, native 37.3-47.9) and G-Man in the train corridor (52-60 s, native 53.6/59.1). ## Current session 55 (2026-11-12)

Claude Code desktop, `claude-opus-5-5`. No subagents. Branch `wip/session55-20261112` off `wip/session54-20261111` (3bfb0d9); main fast-forwarded to 3bfb0d9 after CI 43080829129 passed (windows, macos-15). Labels as before: **SDK**, **retail**, **owned**, **native**, **packaged**. Owner verdict on session 54: the gravity gun's glow now looks good and the weapon bob is "awesome". Details: DESIGN 14v.
- **(a) Static decals that share a material were dropped (owned; native; packaged).** Session 54's "floor infodecals do not render" was wider: a native A/B at the plaza poster wall (artifacts/s55/posters-ab.png) showed a wall poster missing too (decal_posters001a at -3192 -2300 163), while decal_posters002a on the same wall faces, with the same VMT, format and lightmap page, drew. A temporary count of the host's decal draws found 71 for about 160 placed infodecals: 2 of the 48 leaf decals, 2 of the 8 decal_posters001a. Cause: `append_models_lod` merged the world's surfaces into a map keyed by (material, lightmap page, skybox) with `collect()`, which keeps only the last surface of a key; BSP faces are already one surface per key, but `static_decal` makes one surface per decal and receiving surface, so every decal sharing a material and lightmap page with a later one vanished. Breen's backdrop drew because it is the only decal of its material. Fixed: `Surface::batch` (modkit-core) appends the triangles of surfaces sharing a key, and `append_models_lod` uses it (its size baseline lowered). Packaged at d1_trainstation_02 (artifacts/s55/decals-fix.png): the centre poster cluster matches native, the plaza leaf litter (2114 triangles, 27 before) and the trash decals show. Draw count unchanged (1835 meshes); triangles 1064474 to 1067691. Frame time: four alternating 1080p uncapped runs at the plaza (artifacts/s52/plaza-empty.json) gave 45.6/52.1 ms before and 56.7/29.0 ms after: desktop noise far above any effect of 3217 triangles in existing draws, so not measured.
- **(b) The spawn lamp's warm haze is its halo (SDK; retail strings; native; packaged).** Native at the spawn with the point_spotlights on and then off (`ent_fire point_spotlight lightoff`; artifacts/s55/native-lamp-onoff.png): the spotlight adds a warm glow peaking just under the shade (+135, +116, +79 on screen), on average +63/+69/+65/+34/+12 (red) in rings of 0-40/40-100/100-200/200-350/350-600 pixels around that peak. So native draws the halo there; session 53's reason for turning ours off (that the occlusion query hides it inside the shade) was wrong. SDK DrawBeamWithHalo dims the halo by PixelVisibility_FractionVisible: a proxy square facing the view, 1 + scale x width / end width units (clamped to 8) from its center to its corners and that far nearer the view, whose visible share of pixels the halo approaches over 0.0625 s (r_pixelvisibility_partial, a convar retail client.dll also has). Ours now samples that square with five rays (new halo_visibility.rs; the scanners' halos use it too). With the SDK's halo scale 60 our lamp's added light measured 151/126/97/42/10 in those rings (2.4 to 0.85 times native) and whiter (G/R 0.96, B/R 0.82 against native's 0.85 and 0.55, the rendercolor's), so the point_spotlight halo stays off (HALO_SCALE 0) for now (artifacts/s55/lamp-halo-ab2.png). Likely cause (SDK read, not tested): sprite_dx9.cpp defaults `$nosrgb` to 1 for every sprite, and sprite_ps2x.fxc then writes texture x vertex color x $hdrcolorscale through FinalOutput(TONEMAP_SCALE_GAMMA) with no sRGB conversion: sprites add in gamma space after tonemapping. Ours adds the halo in linear light before tonemapping (effects.wgsl's dim-beam path), which on a dark background brightens and whitens far more.
- **(c) The lampbeam proxy (SDK; owned):** sprites/glow_test02.vmt binds `lampbeam`, which sets the beam's $alpha to 1 - |z| of the direction from the view to the beam's start: a beam seen from straight below or above vanishes. Ours did not apply it; now both the lamps' and the scanners' beams do. At the spawn view it changed the lamp's added light by under one level (that beam is dim there).
- **(d) I/O targets fall back to classnames (SDK):** CEventQueue::ServiceEvents gives an event whose target names no entity to every entity of that class (FindEntityByClassname); ours dropped it. In now (`entities::io` targets; unit test). Found because `ent_fire point_spotlight LightOff` did nothing in ours. Map I/O that names a class reaches it too.
- **(e) Static prop decals land on the drawn mesh (SDK; packaged).** Source's fx_impact.cpp Impact gives a static prop hit to AddDecalToStaticProp with the shot's ray continued 8 units past the trace's end, and the studio decal paints the model's triangles along that ray. Ours took only a drawn face within 4 units of the VPhysics hull, so the plaza tree (its trunk well inside its hull) took 1 decal of 7. Now a shot with a direction that hits a static prop moves its hit along the shot onto the nearest front-facing triangle of a nearby static prop's own mesh, from 4 units before the hull hit to 8 past it (new static_prop_decals.rs; unit test). Packaged, session 53's tree fixture (artifacts/s53/bench-tree.json): 7 decals created and 0 unclippable (1 and 6 before); the capture is too blurred by the impact dust to see the marks. Not compared with native. Not in: the studio decal's spread over neighbouring triangles within its radius (ours needs the ray itself to meet a triangle).
- **Validated (e):** artifacts/s55/validate3.sh on the build with it: hl2-simulation's 773 tests passed (strict Clippy for hl2-simulation exit 0 before), fmt and architecture exit 0, replays 72 of 72 in 559 s with JOBS=3. The workspace-wide tests and regression batch were not rerun for it (only hl2-simulation changed).
- **Validated (b)-(d):** artifacts/s55/validate2.sh: strict Clippy, fmt, architecture and build exit 0; 1667 tests passed and the 2 known --release should_panic rendering tests failed; regression 26/26, 17/17, 26/26 (s55-regression2); replays 72 of 72 in 552 s with JOBS=3 (the classname fallback changed no replay outcome).
- **(f) Frame time: dense report labels distort it (packaged).** The plaza profile fixtures from session 52 label every 3 ticks; each label samples the report and the `diagnostics` scope then spikes to 370-550 ms every few seconds (four runs: 29 to 57 ms a frame). With sparse labels (artifacts/s55/plaza-sparse.json) the same view measures 17.1 ms mean, p95 24 ms, max 44 ms at 1080p uncapped (docs/performance.md). fixed_simulation (5.2 ms mean) and render_graph (5.5 ms) lead.
- **(g) Screen shake (SDK; owned maps; native; packaged).** There was none: 154 env_shakes in 42 installed maps (d1_trainstation_01's train rumble and stop, d1_eli_01's lab shake) did nothing. New screen_shake.rs follows CViewEffects::Shake/CalcShake (each shake's random offset within +-amplitude and roll within +-amplitude/4, re-rolled `frequency` times a second, times fraction^2 x sin(time x frequency / fraction), its amplitude decaying by frametime / (duration x frequency): ours uses that drop's continuous limit so the host can read the shake at any time; at most 32 shakes; Stop clears them; Amplitude and Frequency retune the one that ends last) and UTIL_ScreenShake's falloff (amplitude x (1 - distance / radius) to the player's centre, capped at 16; radius 0 with spawnflag Everyone). env_shake takes StartShake, StopShake, Amplitude and Frequency (the latter two kept for later starts). The host adds the offset and roll to the view (ApplyShake factor 1) and gives the viewmodel a tenth more (view.cpp places it from the shaken eye, then CalcViewModelView's ApplyShake factor 0.1). Native d1_eli_01 with eli_lab_shake at amplitude 16 (artifacts/s55/native-shake.png): the view rolls and shifts while the .357 stays almost fixed on screen; a first version of ours that placed the gun from the unshaken eye (my misreading of ItemPostFrame's call) threw it out of view and was corrected. Packaged d1_eli_01: the view moves (mean difference 12.7 levels from an unshaken run). Not in: the in-air rule (the player off the ground is not shaken unless spawnflag 4), physics and rope shakes (spawnflags 8, 16), SHAKE_START_NORUMBLE (spawnflag 64: SDK 2013's Shake() does not add it; 2 env_shakes), explosions' UTIL_ScreenShake calls. Not compared frame by frame with native.

## Current session 49 (2026-11-07)

Claude Code desktop, `claude-opus-5-5`. No subagents. Branch `wip/session49-20261107` off `wip/session48-20261106`. Then to ccceb6c (run 42425323209, covering c2d0a85, the manhack punt, and docs), after both jobs passed. Labels as in session 48: **SDK**, **retail** (installed binaries), **owned** (installed files), **native** (the running game), **packaged** (our build).
- **How the engine picks a model's cubemap: found (retail engine; DESIGN 14o session 49).** The r_showenvcubemap ConVar led there: its four readers are the model render path, whose lighting setup binds the light cache's cubemap (render context slot 0x14). The cache picks the sample nearest a point by squared distance, ties to the earlier one; the point is the middle of the model's 32 by 32 by 128 cell when the illumination origin reaches it (r_lightcachecenter 1), then that middle at the origin's height, else the origin; an entry keeps its sample, so a model changes sample only by entering another cell. Session 48's "nearest" was right, measured from the wrong point. In: `cache_cell`, `LightingData::cache_point`, a re-pick only on a new cell. Static props use the same field of their own entry; they still do not reflect here (late-loaded models do since 58f4e38, below).
- **Cube mips: in, and they explain nothing seen (owned, packaged).** Every owned sample has 6 levels and we uploaded one. With the chain, on session 48's d1_canals_02 frames: detail over mean 0.95 and 0.85 before, 0.94 and 0.89 after, native 0.76 and 0.85; red over blue 1.15 and 1.37 before, 1.15 and 1.31 after, native 1.40 and 1.52. So the speckle and the cool tint are **not explained**.

- **Faceplate: the hover froze on idle.** Native (d1_town_02, 1617 samples; d1_canals_02, 2331 samples): a hunting scanner plays inspect1 and inspect2 all the time and idles only for the flash, about 1.8 s. Ours held ACT_IDLE. SDK: SCHED_CSCANNER_SPOTLIGHT_HOVER asks for ACT_WALK; owned model has none; TranslateActivity's last resort turns a missing walk into a run and the reverse, and inspect1 and inspect2 are the model's ACT_RUN. That rule is now in `SceneMotor::walk_or_run`, for every class. Owned: inspect1 and inspect2 turn the neck about 20 degrees and the eye about 19 and slide the shields, which is the plate's living motion. - **Faceplate: the flare never closed.** Native: a photograph plays sequence 5 (flare) for 0.67 s and sequence 6 (retract) for 1 s, then idle. Ours went from flare straight to idle. The SDK has no call for this: the model's sequence graph routes it (FindTransitionSequence). `Scanner::settle_flare` runs on every task, so it finishes whatever schedule follows. Packaged, d1_trainstation_02, the photograph fixture: inspect1, flare at 3.45 s, retract at 3.75 s, idle at 4.65 s, then the inspect sequences again. That report samples every 0.3 s, so the timing is coarse, and it fits native's 0.67 s and 1 s.

About 2.4 s after a blind ours reads a mean of 154.9 and native 139.7. Nothing was changed. **Open:** native reaches a flat 255 on some flashes (3 of the 7 caught over two runs), ours peaks at 236.8; samples are 2.5 s apart, so the peak's shape is not known. The SDK's values give at most alpha 216.
- **Open: dim sprites.** Sprites default to NOSRGB and add in gamma space; effects.wgsl converts the product, which leaves a dim color about a third as bright. Only the spotlight's two sprites have the corrected order (w = -1). The others keep theirs until a native comparison says otherwise.

- **36 of the 82 bird hints carry a trigger_multiple that kills the NPC touching it (`!activator,Kill`):** this is how maps get rid of birds. A killed bird keeps its last state in the actor table, so frozen birds with speed set are dead ones; read `trigger_entries` or last_think, not `flying`.
- **Rejected:** flying straight at a hint: five of six street crows wedged against buildings. A wrong diagnosis on the way: after routing, frozen birds near the hint looked stuck in solid; they had been killed by the trigger, as above.
- **Replays on 6a071fe found a regression of the birds: 19 of 72 failed, all on one line, `npc/crow/flap2.wav` not preloaded.** The class had no SOUNDS list for the host to preload, and the crows of those replays' maps take off. Unit tests and the fixtures could not show it. Fixed with `bird::SOUNDS` and a test that every cue a bird plays is on the list. Re-validated on that code: strict Clippy clean, 1450 unit tests passed and 2 failed (the two --release should_panic rendering tests), s46d-regression 26/26, 17/17, 26/26, replays 70 of 72 in 435 s with JOBS=4: `monitors-kleiner-scene` (8 gibs removed against 7, the known flake) and `entities-weapons`. ## Current session 40 (2026-10-31)

Claude Code desktop, `claude-opus-5-5`. No subagents. Branch `wip/session40-20261031` off `wip/session39-20261030` (not on main). Plan/status: DESIGN 14o session 40. Fixtures artifacts/s40 (make_fixtures.py, run.sh, summ.py <report> <entity>). Owner playtest reports of this session, each answered in chat first:
- **Gas cans and propane tanks never set NPCs on fire. Fixed.** CBreakableProp::Break for onbreak explode_fire props ignites (Ignite(30)) every NPC whose bounds touch the explode radius (gas can 80, propane 50) and that passes the damage filter, after the blast; only AllowedToIgnite classes (zombies, headcrabs, cops, Combine soldiers), never the player or citizens. Grenades still ignite only torso splits (50%), as in the SDK. Packaged gascan-ignite: the zombie burns and dies ~9 s later.
- **Burning zombies lacked their scream and animation. Fixed.** The classic zombie's (and torso's) moan patch at pitch 120 for ~30 s (CZombie::Ignite), quieted when the fire goes out, destroyed at death; no pain sounds or idle moans while burning; the walk/idle switch at ignition and the `firewalk` locomotion variant (packaged gascan-burning). Burning cops stand on fire 1.5 s and die (unit test only). Burning Combine soldiers: SCHED_COMBINE_BURNING_STAND (ACT_COMBINE_BUGBAIT, 2 + U(0, 3) s, TASK_COMBINE_DIE_INSTANTLY; the playback-rate randomization is not modeled): packaged gascan-soldier (bugbait_hit once the AI runs, dead 3.8 s later).
- **Sawblades did not cut zombies or kill NPCs and turned freely in the gravity gun. Fixed.** Spin (slice) impact damage for spin_zaxis props (npcAngularTable on DotProductAbs(inertia kg m^2, local deg/s), DMG_SLASH), IsChopped -> DieChopped with E3_Phystown.Slicer and a live crab half the time, the 5000 deg/s launch spin without angular drag, and the preferred carry angles (level in the view; harpoon-style preferred_carryangles too). Packaged saw-launch (chopped), saw-hold (level).
- **Owner follow-up: native blades nail into walls. Done.** HandleInteractionStick: a launched blade's first world hit over 1000 u/s, head-on enough, not metal/grate/sky, embeds it 8 units, frozen, until the gravity gun or +USE pulls it out. Packaged saw-wall (stuck level in the stone pillar).
- **Poison zombie blast kills left all nest crabs alive (native: mostly one). Fixed by the SDK rule.** HeadcrabFits ignores only the new crab and the zombie, so the first ejected crab (or a released head crab) blocks the other nest spots; the head crab is now released before the nest evacuates (SDK order). poison-blast and poison-kill: one crab each (before: three). Not native-compared; the "crab already jumping off survives" impression is not what the SDK does (a crab in the zombie's hand at death just vanishes).
- **Panicking crabs merged into one spot. Addressed as native does it.** Native crabs pass through each other too (HL2COLLISION_GROUP_HEADCRAB), and Source never steers one crab around another: the local navigator steers only when the direct move is blocked, and a crab's move traces skip other crabs (CTraceFilterNav uses the mover's collision group). They stay apart by the one-crab nest rule (above), random eject directions and FindCoverNode's 1 s node lock (ported, shared by all NPCs). (The first answer in chat blamed the missing plane solver; corrected after reading MoveCalcRaw.)
- **The crosshair turned white with the gravity gun. Fixed.** weapon_physcannon and weapon_bugbait were missing from the HUD's weapon-script list (both use the Crosshairs "Q"); hl2-ui hud.rs list only (PR #4 touches hl2-ui; one-line conflict risk).

Still different: native ragdolls keep burning ~6 s after death (burning ragdolls, step 6).

Native holds the blade level in front of the view, and the launched blade cuts the zombie into legs and a torso ("died to player"); this roll kept the crab on the torso (ours: the live-crab half). Same outcome. Not compared: the torso's flight (ours throws it high; native landed it a few feet past the legs, one sample each).

## Current session 39 (2026-10-30)

Claude Code desktop, `claude-opus-5-5`. No subagents. Branch `wip/session39-20261030` off `wip/session38-20261030` (not on main). Plan/status: DESIGN 14o session 39. Fixtures artifacts/s39 (run.sh, head_summ.py, cupcop-look, cupcop-look-before = the session 38 build, cupcop-front, cupcop-burst).
- **Owner playtest: NPCs hiccup every few seconds (the can cop's head turns sharply and resets; native does not). Fixed.** SCHED_IDLE_STAND repeats every 5 s and our TASK_SET_ACTIVITY restarted ACT_IDLE each time, so batonidle1 (a 10 s loop) jumped back to frame 0 every 5.04 s. TASK_SET_ACTIVITY is now SetIdealActivity: the running activity keeps its sequence unless the weighted re-roll picks another one. Also new: Source's client sequence blending (CSequenceTransitioner): any base sequence change or restart of an NPC fades the old sequence out over min(fadeout, fadein) (0.2 s for police idles), so idle switches, walk/idle changes and gestures no longer pop. cupcop-look: before, restarts at 4.08/9.12/14.16/19.2 s; after, batonidle1 plays on to 15.7 s of sequence time; the remaining two changes (map-start walk/itemhit and the AI taking over from the script at 4.07 s) blend. The live burst (cupcop-burst) is too dark to judge the blend by eye; the continuity is unit-tested instead.

Source clears too-close/too-far whenever any attack is possible (CAI_BaseNPC::GatherAttackConditions); ours never did, for any NPC. After the fix the same run leaps a crab off its back at 9.0 s, then chases and claws.
- **Black headcrab panic and flinch hops (CBlackHeadcrab):** damage flinches with a hop (flinch1's event), then it panics 2-5 s, scurries for cover (the Scurry run, ~4x faster) and lurks in cover (SCHED_HEADCRAB_AMBUSH); a nest crab ejected at the poison zombie's death panics 2-8 s on landing; a failed cover run after the panic time chases. NPC memory bits (INCOVER, FLINCHED) are new and shared. Packaged blackcrab-flinch and poison-kill (DESIGN 14o session 39). Ambush not shown packaged (the hall has no cover node for the tiny hull).
- **HUD poison indicator (CHudPoisonDamageIndicator):** while the player's m_bPoisoned holds (DMG_POISON pending, poison not all restored, under 100 health: `PlayerDamage::hud_poisoned`) the hudlayout panel HudPoisonDamageIndicator fades in with the owned PoisonDamageTaken/PoisonLoop/PoisonPulse sequences and shows valve_english.txt's Valve_HudPoisonDamage (NEUROTOXIN DETECTED / ADMINISTERING ANTIDOTE); PoisonDamageCured fades it. - **Owner playtest 1: a frame freeze whenever a zombie's crab came off (worse with live crabs). Fixed.** The crab release kept a second handle on the map's World while it created the crab and the headless corpse, so adding the entity copied the whole map (`world_copies` was 1 in every session 37 release run; the torso split's legs had the same pattern). `--profile` now reports `max_ms` and the slowest samples per CPU stage: crab-release-body's kill tick took 449 ms before, 13 ms max after (rel-before/rel-after reports), world copies 1 -> 0, no render stage above 19 ms.
- **Owner playtest 2: the player stuck after a headcrab leap reached him. Fixed.** The airborne crab moved first and only then checked the player's box, so it ended inside him, landed at his feet and every player move started solid (the 2-unit unstick nudges cannot clear a 24-unit crab). Now the player's box is solid in the crab's fall trace like Source's PhysicsTryMove: the touch bites, the crab slides down beside him (ClipVelocity) or, landing on his head, drops off his side (changed with the throws; first it rested there), and a crab a tick-order overlap leaves inside his box is pushed out sideways first. crab-stuck (crab leaps at the hall start, then strafes/forward/back): old build stuck from the third bite to the end (t280-end) with the crab inside his box; fixed build never stuck and walks 130+ units. A crab on the ground can still overlap him ~3 units for a few ticks (the player moves against NPC colliders posed before this tick's NPC moves; it did not trap him).
- **Owner note: poison bites lower the health the recovery returns to.** Already the SDK model (bite = 5 slash, permanent, then poison to one health, restored +10 per 2 s); now `PlayerDamage::poison_bite` in hl2-simulation with a repeated-bite test: 95, 90, ... 10, 5, then one health for good; a bite during recovery at 21 health lowers the target to 90. Packaged blackcrab-town2 gives the same health sequence as session 37.

Source's player physics shadow keeps props out of the box (physics detail, later).

The poison zombie now throws: RangeAttack1/2Conditions, the first throw 1-2 s after a new enemy, a throw at once after a hit of 20% of max health, the SelectCombatSchedule order (backs away when a throw is too close), the threat and throw sequences, pickup (the nest crab disappears, a crab appears in its hand), the throw of a npc_headcrab_poison at the enemy's eyes (ThrowAt: up to 400 high, 1000 u/s), 4-10 s between throws. - **Shotgun corpses ~33% long: the death force went to the wrong solid. Improved.** Native probe (oracle, front-facing cop spawned with sk_metropolice_health 100, one shell from 137 units): health 100 -> 44, all seven pellets hit, so missed pellets do not explain it. Our m_nForceBone was the solid whose origin is nearest the last pellet's hit point; for an off-centre chest pellet that is the 2.5 kg left upper arm, whose share of a 7-pellet impulse exceeds the 2000 in/s velocity limit, so the shotgun and the 357 both saturated (equal distances although the shotgun's force is 16% smaller). CAI_BaseNPC::TraceAttack stores `m_nForceBone = ptr->physicsbone`: the solid of the hit hitbox's bone. Now `RayHit.bone` carries the hit studio hitbox's bone, apply_hit records it per NPC (`npc_force_bones`), and ragdoll creation pushes the solid that simulates that bone (its own, else the nearest ancestor's; the nearest-origin rule stays for hits without a hitbox). Report `ragdolls.last_force_body` = [used, nearest]. Front-facing 40 HP: shotgun 132 -> 106 units (native 95.5-99.2; force solid Spine2 instead of L_UpperArm), 357 125 -> 127 (native 122.5; Spine2 either way). 1 HP (session 32 A/B fixtures): shotgun 107 -> 96 (native ~83, one spread). The pistol at 1 HP is now 40.5 units (session 32: 29.7, native ~11; its force solid is unchanged, so sessions 33-35 moved it: open).
- **Like-for-like kill distances (session 35b):** the same top-down view and track2.py (corpse spot by background subtraction) for native and ours; native cops spawn at 1 HP with `sk_metropolice_health 1` (no SetHealth turn: the private research notes), ours artifacts/s35/bt-*, b-pkf-* against artifacts/s34/pk-background.png. Shotgun 40 HP: native 95.5, 99.2, 95.8 / ours 107.6 (session 34 build: 127.0). 357 40 HP: native 122.5 / ours 129.0 (session 34: 126.9).
- **Ragdoll constraint torque (session 34 open item): read, not changed.** Retail vphysics InitRagdoll (FUN_1000b890, found through its DEG2RAD limit conversions) puts the raw .phy friction (the vcollide parser FUN_10028df0 stores xfriction as the axis torque; police 0.2) times the reference solid's GetMass() into the IVP constraint template (RagdollCreate passes the child as the reference), while SetAngularMotor (FUN_1000c820 -> FUN_10031810) writes friction x DEG2RAD straight into the constraint's six axis fields. The template's conversion into those fields is not read. Trial (rejected): initial joint friction = child mass x friction made every kill longer (shotgun 40 HP 107.6 -> 116.2, 1 HP 102.2 -> 115.7, 357 129.0 -> 131.5, pistol 30.7 -> 31.1; native ~97 / 92.2 / 122.5 / 35.8), so ours keeps friction x DEG2RAD until the first animated-friction think (one 15 ms step; native's first client frame is shorter).

## Current session 35 (2026-10-28)

Claude Code desktop, `claude-opus-5-5`. No subagents. New branch `wip/session35-20261028` on top of `wip/session34-20261028`, merged into main (c83748f). Plan/status: DESIGN 14n session 35 (ragdoll rest). Private evidence: work/hl2-decompiled/npc-gunfire-20261021/lab/s35-*.txt (retail client.dll FX_AffectRagdolls FUN_1012ffa0, CRagdollLRURetirement::Update FUN_101aa3a0, CheckSettleStationaryRagdoll FUN_101a5390, ragdoll_sleepaftertime ConVar 0x104d6050).
- **Owner playtest of session 34: bullets and the crowbar only moved corpses at the moment of death. Fixed.** Native (owner, 2026-10-28): three cop corpses still react to bullets and the crowbar minutes later. FX_AffectRagdolls (SDK and retail) refuses a shot's push while more than one ragdoll is simulating (root awake); native IVP rests a landed corpse moments after it stops, so that window is the tumble right after a multi-kill, and a resting (asleep) body still reacts: any hit wakes it. Our ragdolls never rested: Rapier needs every solid under 0.5 rad/s for 2 s, and a landed police corpse keeps one limb jittering at up to ~0.9 rad/s (artifacts/s35 c-two-sleep, `activity` diagnostics), so with two corpses down every later hit was refused (c-two-sleep: both "simulating" for 16 s). This dates from session 32 (post-death pushes arrived with the native rule): a pre-34 build (a8d41f3, built in a scratch worktree) refused all 10 shots with two corpses (c-two-shots-pre34); session 34's 37x weaker joint friction adds limb jitter. Fix: (1) CheckSettleStationaryRagdoll/ResetRagdollSleepAfterTime (retail: 1.0 unit per axis per update, 5 s, then clear velocities and sleep every solid); (2) ragdoll solids rest under 2 rad/s for 0.5 s (tuned; IVP's deactivation constants are not read). Packaged: two corpses rest 3.2 s after the kill (~1 s after landing); with two corpses down 10 of 11 later pistol shots push (the refusal came while both still tumbled); single-corpse pistol and crowbar 8/8 (c-pistol, c-crowbar). Push size per hit (~1-2 units of root travel for a pistol shot into a lying corpse) is not compared with native.
- Rejected/withdrawn: the session 32 rd-corpse-shots fixture no longer measures pushes (death poses moved the corpse 70 units, its fixed aim passes over it); re-aimed fixtures replace it. A pre-34 worktree build that shared this checkout's target directory left stale modkit-core artifacts here (cleaned; do not share target dirs between worktrees).

The merged tree equals the session branch, so the branch's tests and regression stand for main; session 35's fix is not yet playtested by the owner. (3) Front-facing A/B, 40 HP, one shot from 137 units: 357 native 122.5 units / ours 127; shotgun native 95.5, 99.2 and 95.8 (a fourth run rejected: the cop survived) / ours ~25 before the fix (our pellet batch applied each pellet separately, so the cop died on the fifth and the ragdoll got only the last pellets' force), 127-133 after (`hit_batch`: a target the batch kills takes every pellet's summed force on the last pellet's solid, ApplyMultiDamage -> one TakeDamage); SMG grenade: both corpses leave the 245-unit top view (ours 274). Shotgun is now ~33% long; native is consistent across spreads, so this is not spread variance (open).

Re-test (test-inputs/bevy-tictactoe-use-spins-block, artifacts/s34/ttt): the used block now spins briefly and settles at ~170 degrees within ~1 s (session 16/17: spun at the 3600 deg/s cap for over 6 s); native turns it about a quarter turn and settles within 0.3 s. Then env_physexplosion aims at BodyTarget -> WorldSpaceCenter (the collision bounds' center; ours used the mass center, 2 units off the hinge axis: session 17; SDK physobj.cpp): the block turns smoothly to ~176 degrees by ~1 s and the neighbour block (which also turned ~130 degrees) stays put; native's quarter turn is still not matched (open). The ragdoll constraint init's own conversion is not read (assumed DEG2RAD). Effect on the 1 HP 357/shotgun fixtures: 140/119 -> 127/118 (poses) -> 124/103 (friction).

## Current session 32 (2026-10-26)

Claude Code desktop, `claude-opus-5-5`. No subagents. New branch `wip/session32-20261026` on top of `wip/session31-20261022` (neither is on main; main = d9295d5). Plan/status: DESIGN 14n session 32. Private research notes: work/hl2-decompiled/ragdolls-20261026 (retail decompiles and native logs).
- **Owner playtest of session 31 (2026-10-26), ragdolls.** (1) **Ragdolls blocked the player: fixed.** The player's hull trace has a separate convex SAT path whose collision-group exclusion list missed the ragdoll group (the Rapier filter already excluded it). Unit test: a player hull sweep through a ragdoll is unobstructed (it failed before the fix). Packaged rd-walk: the player walks from y -2520 to -2155 straight through a cop corpse at -2352. (2) **Death force ("only the revolver punches"):** SDK ammo forces are as before (357 BULLET_IMPULSE(800, 5000) ~ 10,900 vs pistol/SMG/AR2 (200, 1225) ~ 670 per bullet: 16x, by design). The shotgun was wrong: only the killing pellet counted. Now FireBullets' multidamage order: pellets on one target add up and apply when a pellet hits a different target (AddMultiDamage -> ApplyMultiDamage) or after the last pellet; pellets after a kill in the same shot pass through the corpse and add their force to it. Owner note: session 31 already let pellets through lined-up cops (damage per pellet, hitboxes off at death); my intermediate trace-all-first version blocked them and was never shipped. Packaged rd-lineup: one shotgun shot kills two lined-up cops. The force solid is m_nForceBone (the last bullet hit's solid; the root for blasts without one); blasts add CalculateExplosiveDamageForce's and CalcDamageForceVector's random fudges; crossbow kills carry CalculateMeleeDamageForce x 0.7 (21,000). (3) **Ragdolls react after death:** client ragdoll effects per tick: FX_AffectRagdolls for every bullet/club trace (4000 kg in/s on the root solid at start + fraction x (hit - start); skipped for ragdolls created this tick and while more than one ragdoll simulates), C_TEExplosion::AffectRagdolls ((dir x (magnitude - magnitude/radius x dist) + 32 up) x 500 at the root, in line of sight). **Retail differs from the 2013 SDK source:** client.dll C_ClientRagdoll::ImpactTrace (FUN_100a5510) scales blasts by 500, not 4000; the shot constant (4000) and the double-fraction point match. Packaged: rd-corpse-shots 8 pushes (corpse moves 14 units over 8 pistol shots); rd-corpse-frag 1 blast push, the corpse is thrown ~500 units by a frag ~40 units away (velocity clamp 2000 u/s; **not native-verified**). Ragdolls stay out of every server trace and projectile sweep (no meat shields). (4) **Crossbow pinning:** SDK client/c_stickybolt.cpp StickRagdollNow / CRagdollBoltEnumerator, confirmed in retail client.dll (FUN_1023cf20: MASK_SHOT clip, mass x2, CreateBallsocketConstraint, Weapon_Crossbow.BoltSkewer): every BoltImpact (bolt stuck in the world, also behind a struck NPC) pins the first ragdoll solid within 128 units in front of the wall point with a ball-socket to the world. Ours: `Ragdolls::retire` with a frustum-only out-of-view test (the view-cluster PVS test is not modeled; burning ragdolls are not exempt yet), the host blends the NPC's materials (`present_ragdoll_fades`; the eyes do not fade). Unit test: ten corpses, the one behind the player fades first, then the oldest; both released after ~0.43 s. Packaged rd-fade (ten cops killed in one tick with `ent_fire c1 sethealth 0`): the two oldest fade (all in view) and are released ~0.43 s later (active 10 -> 8). SetHealth on NPCs now lowers health to zero through the entity damage path (InputSetHealth -> TakeDamage DMG_GENERIC), so it kills like the SDK (before, it set health 0, which our NPC damage reads as "unset").

- **Performance report (@OrbitalMan: FPS ~4 while the can cop chased him to the street ladder): cause found and fixed.** Owned measurement (`assets::owned_route_cost`, d1_trainstation_02, routes from the can cop's post to all 386 graph nodes): one failed route took ~1.9 s (1002 link probes); successful long routes took 0.3-1.6 s. Our A* validated every expanded link with a stepped hull `ground_move` (~1.9 ms each). The nodes south of the street (-3744..-3616, -5264..-5088) are disconnected from the cop's component, so each failed chase swept the whole component, and the cop re-requested about 10 times a second (SCHED_CHASE_ENEMY <-> WARN_TARGET). SDK ports: (1) CAI_Pathfinder::IsLinkUsable uses link data only. Links an NPC fails to walk are marked stale (MarkCurWaypointFailedLink: 4 s) and rechecked once per plan (IsLinkStillStale/CheckStaleRoute: +1 s while still blocked); route doors are probed when the actor reaches the segment. Difference: ours does not mark links on player blocks (the SDK marks everything but NPC blocks); the player-obstruction hold/retry is kept. (2) RememberUnreachable/IsUnreachable (3 s, 120 units) on TASK_GET_PATH_TO_ENEMY/_LKP failures; the chase task chains them by the 300-unit LKP rule; ForceChooseNewEnemy; COND_ENEMY_UNREACHABLE (on a node network); BestEnemy puts reachable enemies first; an unreachable unseen enemy whose LKP is out of sight is eluded. After: worst failed route 10.5 ms, no link probes; packaged can-cop catwalk chase (artifacts/s31/cancop-catwalk): about 10 plan requests per second (~6 ms each) before, one per ~3 s after. cancop-throw-dense, retarget-zombie and retarget-soldier keep their session 30 outcomes (3 baton hits, 8 claws, soldier retarget), and mean step time fell by ~25% (5.8 -> 4.5 ms, 6.1 -> 4.6, 7.1 -> 5.3). Not reproduced: the exact 4 FPS frame on the report's machine (my two packaged placements, catwalk and ladder well, cost ~6 ms per request or lost sight; the owned measurement is the evidence). - **Particle operators from the SDK's compiled particle library (prompt step 4):** the Source SDK 2013 clone ships `particles.lib` (x64, Valve's compiled particle code; object files keep symbols). The operator objects were linked into an analysis-only DLL with a map (private work/hl2-decompiled/particles-20261022/README.md) and decompiled by address; the DMX unpack tables give each parameter's default and field offset. Ported into `pcf_runtime`: Radius Scale (only particles in [start, end) change; easing replaces the bias; Schlick bias f/((1/b-2)(1-f)+1), not the power form), Alpha Fade In/Out Random (a per-particle random fade time r^exponent x (max-min)+min, not the midpoint; proportional defaults on; fade-in spline unless "fade in curve exponent" is set; fade-out spline by default ("ease in and out" defaults to 1), else 1 - bias), Color Fade (skipped when start == end), Movement Basic drag ((1-drag)^(30 dt), frame-rate independent; the exp/log reading is inferred from the call shape). Packaged barrel-burn (artifacts/s31/burn-s30-vs-s31.png): narrower, taller column than session 30, closer to the native frame; single random frames, not frame-verified. The library is the 2025 SDK build; byte equality with the retail client.dll is **not verified**. Position on Model Random now follows the SDK's CParticleSystemQuery::GetRandomPointsOnControllingObjectHitBox (game/shared/particlesystemquery.cpp; C_INIT_CreateOnModel passes "force to be inside model" as the try count and "hitbox scale"/"direction bias"): the best of tries + 1 random hitbox points scored U(0,72) + dot(point - CP, bias) + 1000 inside; our hitbox is still the model's world bounds (one box). Native A/B (artifacts/s31/burn-native-vs-s31.png: native ignite frame vs ours before/after) is **inconclusive**: the native frame was taken right at ignition (few young particles) and the barrels sit at different distances; ours looks larger relative to the barrel. - **Owner gunfire notes, far layer: root cause found and fixed (a782904).** Every new spatial sound player started at volume 0 and got its distance gain one frame later, when its sink existed. The far (right) layer of the `^` NPC SMG1/AR2/pistol waves is a single ~25 ms crack (npc_smg1_fire1.wav: left = 125 ms shot, right = crack), so far shots were silent: owner street layout (soldier spawned at the station-entrance end, walking player at the pallet-launch spot (-2935,-4431), ~1320 units, all shots `#dv16`), per-process recording before the fix: median -99 dB on the far-only stretch; after: far shots audible, far/near p99 difference -2.4 dB (native -1.7 dB). Players now start at volume x dist_gain. Retail evidence (engine.dll, Ghidra): the '^' mixers use left = near, right = far (confirms ours); SND_PickDynamicChannel keeps a same-source CHAN_WEAPON sound that has a delayed start (every WeaponSound with a soundtime) and stops it at the new sound's start sample, so native also cuts each NPC SMG1 shot at 0.075 s (not the cause of the "rat-tat-tat"). The near "saw" vs "rat-tat-tat" report is plausibly the same bug (each shot's loudest first frame was muted every 75 ms); the measurement did not separate it (our shot alignment was unreliable): **owner listening needed** (clips in the private notes).

## Current session 26 (2026-10-21)

Claude Code desktop, `claude-opus-5-5`. No subagents. Branch `wip/runtime-entities-20261021` (from `wip/npc-ai-20261014`), not on main. Owner priority: DESIGN 14r runtime entity spawning.
- **Runtime spawns of loaded classes (DESIGN 14r part A, done):** F4/npc_create creates a new entity copied from the class's hidden template (the old pool slots, never shown now) instead of activating a slot, so any number can be spawned. The map's World grows in place (`Gameplay.world` is the only long-lived Arc; `world_copies` stays 0), so every lookup by entity id works for spawned ids. Physics (hull, studio hitboxes), AI actor, movement controller, eyes and draws (shared GPU meshes, per-entity lit materials/flex meshes, own skeleton, bone-merged weapons) are copied from the template. Bug found on the way: the AI's NPC sight-candidate list was built once on first use, so NPCs spawned later were never seen (fixed; unit test fails without it). Limit: Source's MAX_EDICT_BITS 11 (2048 edicts) is compiled into the engine and its network entity handles; ours defaults to 2048 live runtime spawns and `--max-edicts N` changes it (0 = none). Raising it only breaks Valve wire compatibility, which HL2-RS does not have; a future HL2-RS protocol should send ids as 32-bit/varint with a generation and announce the server's limit (DESIGN 14r).
- **npc_template_maker repeat spawns (done):** the second and later spawns of a maker (or a second maker of the same template) are new entities copied from the held template (SDK MakeNPC parses the template data every time): SF_NPC_TEMPLATE removed, fall flag per the maker, OnSpawnNPC with the child as activator, MaxNPCCount/MaxLiveChildren respected. Packaged d1_canals_01: barrelpush_cop3_maker's first child killed, Spawn -> copy 1397 dropped to the floor at the maker; a third Spawn refused (MaxNPCCount 2). Makers whose spawn box is occupied, or HIDEFROMPLAYER makers in view, refuse like native.

(2) The far layer of distance-variant gunfire is not heard (the near layer only fades): check the premix choice and the SNDLVL_GUNFIRE attenuation at > 1320 units against native recordings. (3) NPC footsteps: done above. (4) NPC chatter: done above for cops/soldiers (squad lines and citizens open). Leading hypothesis for (1) and (2) (not tested): native's room DSP. SDK CHLMachineGun::WeaponSoundRealtime emits both SINGLE_NPC sounds from the owner on CHAN_WEAPON (the second with a future soundtime), so native also restarts the attack every 0.075 s (session 24's run level agrees); our cadence and replacement match, but we play every sound dry. Retail SND_Spatialize also computes a distance-dependent dspmix (channel +0xfc, next to distmix +0x104), and the soundscape/env_soundscape DSP room reverb would smear the 0.075 s restarts into a continuous saw and make far gunfire sound distant. Next: read dspmix (SND_Spatialize 1008f4d0, dsp_dist_min/max) and the DSP preset path in retail engine.dll, then a minimal per-room reverb with distance mix; confirm with native per-process recordings. Retail detail (read this session, private npc-gunfire-20261021 README): dspmix = lerp(dsp_mix_min, dsp_mix_max, (clamp(dist, dsp_dist_min, dsp_dist_max = 1440) - min) / (max - min)), both mixes scaled by dsp_db_mixdrop for sounds at or below dsp_db_min, 0 for '>' doppler waves or dsp_off. Retail defaults: dsp_mix_min 0.2, dsp_mix_max 0.8, dsp_dist_min 0, dsp_dist_max 1440, dsp_db_min 80, dsp_db_mixdrop 0.5: NPC gunfire is 20% reverb up close and up to 80% at 1440+ units.
